## Day 4 - Thursday (Week 1, Day 4)

### Training Load: Medium
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 550 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (12 minutes)
- 5 minutes easy walking/light jogging
- Dynamic warm-up:
  - Leg swings: 10 forward/back, 10 side-to-side per leg
  - Walking lunges with rotation: 10 per leg
  - High knees: 2 x 20 seconds
  - Butt kicks: 2 x 20 seconds
  - Straight leg bounds: 2 x 20 meters

#### Main Workout
- **Type**: Moderate Easy Run with Strides
- **Distance**: 10km
- **Pace/Intensity**: Zone 1-2 (6:00-6:30/km)
- **Details**: First 8km at comfortable easy pace (Zone 1). Final 2km slightly uptempo but still controlled (easy Zone 2). After completion, perform 4 x 100m strides:
  - Gradually accelerate to 80% effort over 100m
  - Focus on form: quick cadence, relaxed upper body
  - Walk/jog 60 seconds recovery between each
  - Purpose: Improve running economy and leg turnover

#### Cool-down (12 minutes)
- 5 minutes easy walking
- Comprehensive stretching:
  - Downward dog: 45 seconds
  - Pigeon pose: 45 seconds each side
  - Seated forward fold: 60 seconds
  - Figure-4 stretch (glutes): 45 seconds each side
  - Calf stretch against wall: 45 seconds each leg

#### Strength Training
**Injury Prevention Circuit** (15 minutes)
Perform after cool-down:
- Calf raises: 3 sets x 20 reps (focus on tibialis anterior)
- Single-leg balance: 3 sets x 45 seconds each leg
- Glute bridges: 3 sets x 15 reps
- Lateral band walks: 3 sets x 12 steps each direction
- Toe walks: 2 sets x 30 meters

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 1, Day 6)

### Training Load: High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,150 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy walking/jogging (7:00+/km)
- Dynamic stretching:
  - Leg swings: 15 each direction per leg
  - Walking lunges: 12 per leg
  - High knees: 2 x 20 seconds
  - Butt kicks: 2 x 20 seconds
  - Hip circles: 10 each direction
  - A-skips: 30 meters
- 5 minutes gradual pace increase to easy running pace

#### Main Workout
- **Type**: Long Run - Aerobic Base Building
- **Distance**: 18km
- **Pace/Intensity**: Zone 2 (5:50-6:15/km)
- **Details**: 
  - First 3km: Settle into comfortable rhythm, Zone 1-2 pace (6:15/km)
  - Km 4-15: Steady Zone 2 pace (5:50-6:10/km), should be able to speak in sentences
  - Final 3km: Maintain effort, allow natural fade if tired
  - **Nutrition during run**: 
    - Gel #1 at 40 minutes (FDC energy gel - 21g carbs)
    - Gel #2 at 1:20 (80 minutes)
    - Water: 150-200ml every 15-20 minutes (carry handheld or use belt)
  - **Purpose**: Build endurance, practice long-run pacing, test nutrition strategy
  - **Mental focus**: Stay relaxed, maintain form, practice positive self-talk

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy walking
- Static stretching (hold each 60 seconds):
  - Calf stretch (both straight and bent knee)
  - Quad stretch
  - Hamstring stretch (seated)
  - Hip flexor stretch (kneeling lunge)
  - Glute stretch (pigeon pose or figure-4)
  - IT band stretch

#### Main Workout
- **Type**: Progressive Long Run
- **Distance**: 20km (milestone run!)
- **Pace/Intensity**: Zone 2 (5:50-6:15/km)
- **Details**:
  - **Km 1-4**: Settle in, Zone 1-2 (6:10-6:20/km) - focus on relaxation
  - **Km 5-16**: Steady Zone 2 (5:50-6:10/km) - find your rhythm, maintain conversation capability
  - **Km 17-20**: Maintain effort, natural slight fade acceptable
  - **Nutrition Strategy**:
    - Gel #1: 45 minutes (FDC energy gel - 21g carbs)
    - Gel #2: 1:30 (90 minutes)
    - Optional Gel #3: 2:00 if needed
    - Water: 200ml every 15-20 minutes
  - **Mental Strategy**:
    - Break into 4 x 5km segments mentally
    - Use mantras if feeling challenged
    - Stay present - don't think about total distance
  - **Purpose**: Build endurance to 20km, practice sustained aerobic running, refine nutrition timing

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy walking

- Monitor how body responded to 20km
- Note any areas of persistent soreness
- Ensure quality sleep was maintained (average 8+ hours)
- Hydration and nutrition timing working well

**Nutrition Highlights**:
- Total weekly calories: ~20,550 kcal
- Successfully matched calories to training demands
- Post-workout nutrition timing improving
- Long run fueling strategy working (2-3 gels)

**Areas of Focus for Week 3**:
- Long run extends to 22km
- Continue developing tempo work
- Maintain injury prevention protocols
- Keep building aerobic base

**Physical Feedback Checklist**:
- Any shin discomfort? (Address immediately if yes)
- Sleep quality adequate?
- Energy levels good during runs?
- Recovery between workouts complete?
- Appetite matching training load?

**Looking Ahead to Week 3**:
- Final week of base building before build phase
- 22km long run - biggest distance yet
- Continue tempo development
- Target: 45km total weekly mileage

## Day 20 - Saturday (Week 3, Day 6)

### Training Load: Very High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,410 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging (7:00+/km)
- Progressive dynamic warm-up:
  - Leg swings: 15 each direction per leg
  - Walking lunges: 15 per leg
  - High knees: 3 x 20 seconds
  - Butt kicks: 3 x 20 seconds
  - Hip circles: 10 each direction
  - A-skips: 40 meters
  - Gradual pace increase to easy running

#### Main Workout
- **Type**: Progressive Long Run - PEAK BASE DISTANCE
- **Distance**: 22km (NEW PERSONAL BEST!)
- **Pace/Intensity**: Zone 2 (5:50-6:15/km)
- **Details**:
  - **Km 1-5**: Settle in gradually (Zone 1-2, 6:10-6:20/km)
    - Focus on relaxation and efficiency
    - Find comfortable breathing rhythm
  - **Km 6-18**: Steady aerobic pace (Zone 2, 5:50-6:10/km)
    - This is your "marathon simulation" effort
    - Maintain ability to speak in sentences
    - Stay mentally present and engaged
  - **Km 19-22**: Hold effort, allow natural fade if needed
    - Last 3km will be challenging - embrace it
    - Practice mental toughness strategies
  - **Nutrition Strategy**:
    - Gel #1: 45 minutes (~7.5km)
    - Gel #2: 1:30 hours (~15km)
    - Gel #3: 2:00 hours (~20km)
    - Water: 200-250ml every 15-20 minutes
    - Total fluid: 1200-1500ml
  - **Mental Strategy**:
    - Break into 5km segments
    - Use mantras: "Strong and steady", "One kilometer at a time"
    - Stay present - don't think about total distance remaining
    - Positive self-talk especially km 18-22
  - **Purpose**: Peak base building distance, mental preparation, nutrition rehearsal

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy walking (don't sit immediately)
- Comprehensive stretching (hold 75-90 seconds each):
  - Hip flexors (kneeling lunge with reach)
  - Hamstrings (seated forward fold, both legs & single leg)
  - Calves (wall stretch, both straight & bent knee)
  - Quads (standing or lying, use wall if needed)
  - Glutes (pigeon pose, figure-4 stretch)
  - IT band (cross-leg forward fold, side-lying)
  - Adductors (butterfly stretch)

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)

  * Walking lunges: 2x10 each leg
  * High knees: 2x20 meters
  * Butt kicks: 2x20 meters

#### Main Workout: Tempo Introduction
- **Type**: Tempo Run
- **Duration**: 45 minutes total
- **Structure**:
  * 15 minutes easy running (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
  * **20 minutes tempo at threshold** (Zone 3: 5:15-5:30/km)
  * 10 minutes easy running to finish (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
- **Total Distance**: ~9-10km
- **Details**: The 20-minute tempo is your introduction to sustained threshold work. Start conservatively at 5:25-5:30/km for the first 5 minutes, then settle into 5:15-5:20/km. This should feel "comfortably hard" - you can speak short sentences but it requires effort. Focus on smooth breathing rhythm and maintaining form.

#### Cool-down (10 minutes)
- 5 minutes very easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 5 minutes walking
- Static stretching:
  * Quad stretch: 30 seconds each leg

## Day 4 - Thursday (Week 5, Day 4)

### Training Load: Medium
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 680 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 5 minutes progressive pace building to Zone 2
- 4 x leg swings (10 each direction per leg)
- 2 x 20m high knees
- 2 x 20m butt kicks

#### Main Workout: Moderate Aerobic Run with Strides
- **Type**: Steady aerobic run with form drills
- **Duration**: 50 minutes
- **Structure**:
  * 40 minutes steady at Zone 2 (5:50-6:15/km)
  * 4 x 100m strides with 90 seconds walk recovery
  * 5 minutes easy cooldown jog
- **Total Distance**: ~8-9km
- **Details**: The steady portion should feel comfortable but purposeful - you're building aerobic capacity. The strides are not sprints; run at about 90% effort for 100m focusing on smooth form, quick leg turnover, and relaxed shoulders. Walk fully between strides to recover. These maintain neuromuscular fitness and running economy.

#### Cool-down (10 minutes)
- 5 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1)
- 5 minutes walking
- Static stretching routine:
  * Standing quad stretch: 30 seconds each leg
  * Downward dog: 45 seconds
  * Pigeon pose: 45 seconds each side
  * Seated hamstring stretch: 30 seconds each leg

#### Strength Training (25 minutes)
Focus on shin splint prevention and running-specific strength:

**Lower Leg Circuit:**
- Calf raises (straight leg): 3 sets x 20 reps
- Calf raises (bent knee): 3 sets x 20 reps
- Tibialis raises (toe raises): 3 sets x 15 reps
- Toe walks: 3 sets x 30 meters
- Heel walks: 3 sets x 30 meters

**Running Strength:**
- Single-leg glute bridges: 3 sets x 12 reps each leg
- Lateral band walks: 3 sets x 15 steps each direction
- Single-leg balance: 3 sets x 45 seconds each leg
- Bulgarian split squats: 3 sets x 10 reps each leg

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 5, Day 6)

### Training Load: High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,680 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Pre-Run Routine (60 minutes before start)
- Bathroom routine
- Light breakfast (see nutrition below)
- Apply anti-chafe products
- Dress appropriately for weather
- Final gear check

#### Warm-up (10 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 3 x 30-second pickups to marathon pace
- 2 minutes walking
- Dynamic stretches:
  * Leg swings: 10 each direction per leg
  * Walking lunges: 10 each leg
  * High knees: 2 x 15 seconds

#### Main Workout: Long Run with Marathon Pace
- **Type**: Endurance run with race pace practice
- **Total Distance**: 26km
- **Duration**: ~2:35-2:45
- **Structure**:
  * **Kilometers 1-8** (45-52 minutes): Easy aerobic pace, Zone 2 (5:55-6:10/km)
    - Focus on settling into rhythm
    - Stay relaxed and controlled
    - Practice nutrition: take gel at 40 minutes
  
  * **Kilometers 9-18** (55-60 minutes): **Marathon Goal Pace**, Zone 5 (5:35-5:42/km)
    - This is your KEY 10km segment at marathon pace
    - Target: 5:38-5:40/km (consistent effort)
    - Focus on smooth breathing and relaxed shoulders
    - Take gel at 1:20 and 1:40
    - This should feel "comfortably challenging"
    - Mental cue: "This is my race pace, it should feel sustainable"
  
  * **Kilometers 19-26** (45-50 minutes): Easy Zone 2 pace (6:00-6:15/km)
    - Consciously slow down after marathon pace segment
    - Finish strong but controlled
    - Take final gel at 2:10
    - Practice finishing with good form when tired

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy walking
- 5 minutes standing, gentle stretching
- No aggressive stretching yet

## Day 2 - Tuesday (Week 6, Day 2)

### Training Load: High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 920 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 12 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- Dynamic stretches:
  * Leg swings: 10 each direction per leg
  * Walking lunges: 2x12 each leg
  * High knees: 2x20 meters
  * Butt kicks: 2x20 meters
  * A-skips: 2x20 meters
  * 4 x 50m gradual accelerations

#### Main Workout: Extended Tempo Run
- **Type**: Threshold/Tempo Run
- **Duration**: 52 minutes total
- **Structure**:
  * 12 minutes easy running (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
  * **30 minutes continuous tempo** (Zone 3: 5:15-5:30/km)
  * 10 minutes easy running to finish (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
- **Total Distance**: ~10-11km
- **Details**: This is a progression from last week's 20-minute tempo. Start the tempo segment conservatively at 5:25-5:30/km for the first 10 minutes. Gradually work down to 5:15-5:20/km for the final 20 minutes. This is lactate threshold pace - comfortably hard. Focus on rhythm and controlled breathing. You should be able to speak short sentences but it requires effort.

**Key Mental Cues:**
- First 10 min: "Controlled and patient"
- Middle 10 min: "Find my rhythm"
- Final 10 min: "Strong and steady"

#### Cool-down (12 minutes)
- 7 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 5 minutes walking
- Static stretching:
  * Hip flexor stretch: 45 seconds each side
  * Quad stretch: 45 seconds each side
  * Hamstring stretch: 45 seconds each side
  * Calf stretch: 45 seconds each side
  * Pigeon pose: 60 seconds each side

## Day 4 - Thursday (Week 6, Day 4)

### Training Load: High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 870 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (20 minutes)
- 15 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1-2: 6:15-6:45/km)
- Dynamic stretches:
  * Leg swings: 12 each direction per leg
  * Walking lunges: 2x12 each leg
  * High knees: 3x20 meters
  * Butt kicks: 3x20 meters
  * A-skips: 3x20 meters
  * B-skips: 2x20 meters
- 4 x 100m strides with full recovery (building to 90% effort)

#### Main Workout: Interval Session - VO2 Max Development
- **Type**: Track/Road Intervals
- **Structure**: 6 x 1km @ Zone 4 pace (4:45-5:00/km) with 400m jog recovery (3 minutes)
- **Total Distance**: ~11-12km including warm-up/cool-down
- **Details**: This is your introduction to structured interval training. Each 1km repeat should be run at approximately 10K race pace (4:50-4:55/km target). The recovery jog should be very easy - don't rush it. Focus on maintaining consistent pace for all 6 repeats.

**Interval Breakdown:**
- Rep 1: 4:55-5:00 (conservative start)
- Reps 2-4: 4:50-4:55 (find rhythm)
- Reps 5-6: 4:45-4:50 (finish strong if feeling good)

**Recovery Between Reps:**
- 400m jog (about 3 minutes at 7:00-7:30/km pace)
- Focus on catching breath and preparing for next rep

**Key Points:**
- Start conservatively
- Even pacing more important than speed
- Better to finish strong than start too fast
- This improves VO2 max and running economy

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 5 minutes walking
- Thorough static stretching:
  * Hip flexors: 60 seconds each
  * Quads: 60 seconds each
  * Hamstrings: 60 seconds each
  * Calves: 60 seconds each
  * IT bands (standing cross-over): 45 seconds each

### Nutrition Plan

**Daily Calorie Target**: 3,250 kcal
**Macros**: 488g carbs (60%) | 146g protein (18%) | 79g fats (22%)

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 6, Day 6)

### Training Load: High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,450 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Pre-Run Routine (60 minutes before)
- Bathroom routine
- Pre-run nutrition (see below)
- Apply anti-chafe products
- Dress for weather
- Final equipment check

#### Warm-up (10 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:30-6:45/km)
- 2 minutes walking
- Light dynamic stretches:
  * Leg swings: 8 each direction
  * Walking lunges: 8 each leg
  * High knees: 2 x 15 seconds

#### Main Workout: Long Run (Recovery Week Prep)
- **Type**: Aerobic endurance run
- **Total Distance**: 22km
- **Duration**: ~2:15-2:20
- **Pace Strategy**:
  * **Kilometers 1-22**: Steady aerobic pace, Zone 2 (5:55-6:10/km)
  * NO marathon pace segments this week
  * Focus on consistent, controlled effort throughout
  * This is intentionally scaled back from last week's 26km

**Details**: This long run is reduced in both distance and intensity as preparation for next week's recovery week. Run the entire distance at comfortable aerobic pace. No need to push - the purpose is maintaining aerobic fitness while allowing recovery from this week's hard tempo and interval sessions. Practice nutrition timing but keep effort conversational throughout.

**Nutrition During Run:**
- Gel 1: 40 minutes (~7km)
- Gel 2: 1:15 (~15km)
- Gel 3: 1:50 (~20km)
- Hydration: Every 15-20 minutes, total ~1.5L

**Mental Approach:**
- Start conservative and relaxed
- Maintain even effort throughout
- Use this as active recovery
- Enjoy the run - no pressure today
- Practice positive self-talk

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 12 minutes easy walking
- 3 minutes standing, breathing
- Light stretching (gentle only)

#### Main Workout: Sustained Tempo Run
- **Type**: Threshold/Tempo Run
- **Duration**: 52 minutes total
- **Structure**:
  * 12 minutes easy running (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
  * **30 minutes continuous tempo** (Zone 3: 5:15-5:30/km)
  * 10 minutes easy running (Zone 2: 6:00-6:15/km)
- **Total Distance**: ~10.5km
- **Details**: Same structure as last week - you should feel more comfortable with this now. Start at 5:25-5:30/km for first 10 minutes, settle into 5:18-5:22/km for middle 10 minutes, finish at 5:15-5:20/km for final 10 minutes. Focus on smooth, controlled breathing and maintaining form as fatigue sets in.

**Mental Approach:**
- "I've done this before, I know I can do it"
- Focus on each 10-minute segment

## Day 4 - Thursday (Week 7, Day 4)

### Training Load: High

### Workout Plan

#### Warm-up (20 minutes)
- 15 minutes easy jogging (Zone 1-2)

  * Leg swings: 12 each direction per leg

  * High knees: 3x20m
  * Butt kicks: 3x20m
  * A-skips: 3x20m
  * B-skips: 2x20m
- 5 x 100m strides (progressive to 90% effort)

#### Main Workout: 800m Intervals
- **Type**: VO2 Max Intervals
- **Structure**: 8 x 800m @ Zone 4 pace (3:45-4:00 per 800m, ~4:40-5:00/km) with 400m jog recovery (2:45-3:00)
- **Total Distance**: ~12-13km
- **Details**: Progression from last week's 1km repeats. Target 3:50-3:55 for each 800m repeat. The shorter distance allows slightly faster pace than 1km repeats. Focus on consistent pacing - first 4 reps should feel controlled, last 4 can push if feeling strong.

**Target Times:**
- Reps 1-2: 3:55 (controlled start)
- Reps 3-6: 3:50-3:52 (rhythm)
- Reps 7-8: 3:45-3:50 (strong finish if able)

**Recovery:**
- 400m jog between each (about 2:45-3:00, very easy)
- Walk if needed to fully recover

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging
- 5 minutes walking
- Comprehensive stretching: 60 seconds each major group

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 7, Day 6)

### Training Load: Very High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,820 kcal

### Workout Plan

#### Main Workout: 28km Long Run with Marathon Pace
- **Type**: Endurance run with race pace work
- **Total Distance**: 28km
- **Duration**: ~2:45-2:55
- **Structure**:
  * **Kilometers 1-10** (58-65 minutes): Easy aerobic, Zone 2 (5:50-6:10/km)
  * **Kilometers 11-22** (65-70 minutes): **Marathon Goal Pace**, Zone 5 (5:35-5:42/km)  
    - This is 12km at marathon pace (progression from 10km last time)
    - Target: 5:38-5:40/km consistent
  * **Kilometers 23-28** (34-38 minutes): Easy Zone 2 (6:00-6:15/km)

**Details**: This is a significant milestone - 28km is your longest run yet, and 12km at marathon pace is substantial race-specific work. The first 10km should feel very comfortable. The marathon pace section should feel challenging but sustainable - this is what 70-80% of your race will feel like. Focus on staying relaxed and maintaining form as fatigue builds.

**Nutrition During Run:**
- Gel 1: 40 minutes
- Gel 2: 1:15
- Gel 3: 1:50
- Gel 4: 2:25
- Hydration: Every 15-20 minutes, total ~2L

- **Type**: Tempo Run

- Static stretching:
  - Calf stretches (30 seconds each leg)
  - Hamstring stretches (30 seconds each leg)
  - Hip flexor stretches (30 seconds each leg)
  - Quad stretches (30 seconds each leg)

- Single-leg calf raises: 3 sets x 15 reps each leg
- Glute bridges: 3 sets x 15 reps
- Lateral band walks: 3 sets x 10 steps each direction

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 9, Day 6)

### Training Load: Very High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 1,850 kcal

### Workout Plan

**CRITICAL SESSION: 30km Long Run with Marathon Pace Work**

#### Pre-Run Routine (60-90 minutes before)
- Wake up 2.5 hours before run start
- Bathroom routine
- Light breakfast (see nutrition below)
- Apply anti-chafe products
- Final hydration check

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:40/km)
- 5 minutes dynamic stretching:
  - Leg swings (10 each direction, each leg)
  - Walking lunges (10 each leg)
  - Hip circles (10 each direction)
  - Ankle mobility exercises

#### Main Workout
- **Type**: Long Run with Marathon Pace Segments
- **Duration**: ~2 hours 45 minutes
- **Distance**: 30km total
- **Pace/Intensity**: Mixed zones
- **Detailed Breakdown**:

**Kilometers 1-5**: Easy warm-up pace
- Zone 1-2: 6:15-6:30/km
- Get comfortable, settle into rhythm
- Check form, breathing, nutrition setup

**Kilometers 6-10**: Steady aerobic pace
- Zone 2: 6:00-6:10/km
- Building into the run
- First gel at 40 minutes (km 7)

**Kilometers 11-25**: MARATHON PACE WORK (15km)
- Zone 5: 5:38-5:42/km (goal race pace)
- This is the CRITICAL segment
- Focus: Controlled, sustainable, confident
- Gel at 60, 80, 100 minutes
- Hydrate every 15-20 minutes
- Mental cues: "Strong and steady" / "This is my pace"
- Practice race-day mindset

**Kilometers 26-30**: Cool down
- Zone 2: 6:10-6:20/km
- Ease off gradually
- Final gel at 2 hours if needed
- Focus on maintaining form despite fatigue

#### Cool-down (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes easy walking immediately after
- Light stretching:
  - Calves, hamstrings, quads, hip flexors
  - Gentle movements only
- Don't sit down immediately - keep moving

#### Fueling During Run
- **Gel schedule**: 
  - Gel 1: 20 minutes (km 3-4)
  - Gel 2: 40 minutes (km 7-8)
  - Gel 3: 60 minutes (km 11-12)
  - Gel 4: 80 minutes (km 15-16)
  - Gel 5: 100 minutes (km 19-20)
  - Gel 6: 120 minutes (km 23-24)
  - Gel 7: 140 minutes (km 27-28)
- **Hydration**: 600-700ml per hour (sports drink with electrolytes)
- Total fluid: ~1.8 liters during run

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 10, Day 6)

### Training Load: PEAK - Very High
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 2,000 kcal

### Workout Plan

**PEAK SESSION: 32km Long Run with 20km Marathon Pace**
*This is your FINAL major training milestone - race dress rehearsal*

#### Pre-Run Routine (2.5 hours before)
- Wake up 2.5 hours before start
- Bathroom routine
- Pre-run breakfast (detailed below)
- Apply anti-chafe products thoroughly
- Double-check all gear and nutrition
- Final hydration check (but don't overdo it)
- Arrive at starting point 15 minutes early

#### Warm-up (15 minutes)
- 10 minutes very easy jogging (Zone 1: 6:40/km)
- Dynamic stretching (5 minutes):
  - Leg swings (10 each direction, each leg)
  - Walking lunges (12 each leg)
  - Hip circles (10 each direction)
  - Ankle mobility exercises
  - A-skips (2 x 10 meters)

#### Main Workout - RACE SIMULATION
- **Type**: Peak Long Run with Extended Marathon Pace
- **Duration**: ~2 hours 50 minutes
- **Distance**: 32km total
- **Pace/Intensity**: Mixed zones with extended race pace

**Detailed Kilometer Breakdown:**

**Kilometers 1-5**: Warm-up
- Zone 1-2: 6:20-6:35/km
- Settle in, find rhythm
- Check all systems (nutrition access, hydration, comfort)
- Stay conservative - long day ahead

**Kilometers 6-11**: Build to steady
- Zone 2: 6:05-6:15/km
- Progressive pace increase
- First gel at 40 minutes (km 7)
- Get comfortable with effort

**Kilometers 12-31**: MARATHON PACE - THE CRITICAL 20KM
- Zone 5: 5:38-5:42/km (GOAL RACE PACE)
- This is IT - your race day pace for 20km
- **This segment is CRUCIAL for race confidence**
- Gel schedule:
  - Gel 2: 60 minutes (km 11)
  - Gel 3: 80 minutes (km 15)
  - Gel 4: 100 minutes (km 19)
  - Gel 5: 120 minutes (km 23)
  - Gel 6: 140 minutes (km 27)
  - Gel 7: 160 minutes (km 30)
- Hydrate every 15-20 minutes (800ml/hour)
- Mental focus points:
  - "This is my pace"
  - "Strong and sustainable"
  - "Building race-day confidence"
  - Practice positive self-talk
- Check form every 2km (relaxed shoulders, efficient stride)

**Kilometer 32**: Cool down finish
- Zone 2: 6:10-6:20/km
- Ease down gradually
- Final gel if needed
- Celebrate completing this massive achievement!

#### Cool-down (15-20 minutes)
- 15 minutes easy walking immediately
- Keep moving - don't sit or stop
- Light stretching (very gentle):
  - Calves, hamstrings, quads, hip flexors
  - Hold each 20-30 seconds only
- Continue walking as needed

#### Race Simulation Elements
**PRACTICE TODAY:**
- Exact race-day clothing
- Race-day nutrition timing
- Goal pace for extended period
- Mental strategies
- Hydration protocol
- Post-gel hydration
- Form maintenance when tired

### Nutrition Plan

**Daily Calorie Target**: 4,200 kcal
**Macros**: 630g carbs | 160g protein | 100g fats

### Hydration Strategy
- Pre-run: 500ml water (90 min before)

- Rest of day: 2.5 liters water
- **Total: 7+ liters**
- Add electrolyte tablets to post-run water
- Monitor urine color every 2 hours

### Recovery & Sleep

**Immediate Post-Run (First 45 minutes)**
- Walk for 15 minutes minimum - DO NOT sit down yet
- Consume immediate recovery nutrition
- Change into dry, warm clothes
- Ice bath: 12 minutes (if available and tolerable)

**First 3 Hours Post-Run**
- Compression socks/tights: wear for 3-4 hours minimum
- Elevate legs: 30-40 minutes total (in intervals)
- Light foam rolling: 20 minutes (VERY gentle pressure only)
- Gentle stretching: 15 minutes (no deep stretching)
- Rehydrate aggressively with electrolytes

**Afternoon/Evening Protocol**
- Foam rolling session #2: 15 minutes (gentle)
- Stretching routine #2: 20 minutes (all major groups)
- Self-massage: feet, calves, quads (10 minutes)
- Epsom salt bath: 25 minutes in warm water
- Continue elevation when sitting
- Ice any specific hot spots (10 minutes max)

**Evening/Night**
- Final gentle stretching: 10 minutes before bed
- Compression garments optional for sleep
- Extra pillow under feet for elevation
- Room cool and dark

**Sleep Priority**
- This is CRITICAL for recovery
- Sleep target: 9-10 hours minimum
- Bed by 9:30 PM
- Sleep in tomorrow if possible
- Quality sleep = quality recovery

**Self-Monitoring Checklist**
- [ ] No sharp pains anywhere
- [ ] Shin area status: Normal / Slightly sore / Concerning
- [ ] Overall energy: Good / Tired (expected) / Exhausted
- [ ] Appetite: Normal / High / Low
- [ ] Mood: Positive / Neutral / Low
- [ ] Any unusual soreness: Yes / No (specify: _______)

**When to Be Concerned:**
- Sharp, localized pain
- Shin pain that's more than minor soreness
- Unusual swelling
- Inability to walk normally
- Fever or illness symptoms

# Week 12 - Taper & Race Week

## Week Overview
- **Focus**: TAPER - Reduce volume by 50%, maintain intensity briefly, allow full recovery and freshness. Final race preparation and RACE DAY!
- **Weekly Mileage Target**: 25-30km (plus 42.2km race)
- **Key Workouts**: Reduced long run (16km), short tempo efforts, easy runs with pickups, final shakeout, MARATHON RACE DAY

---

## Day 1 - Monday (Week 12, Day 1)

### Training Load: Rest Day
**Estimated Calories Burned**: 0 kcal

### Taper Week Begins!
Complete rest. The taper is NOT about losing fitness - it's about arriving at the start line fresh, rested, and ready.

### Taper Mindset
- Trust your training - you've done the work
- Some "phantom" aches are normal in taper
- Resist urge to train more
- Energy levels may fluctuate
- Mental preparation is key now

- Easy walking: 15-20 minutes
- Gentle stretching: 15 minutes
- Light foam rolling: 10 minutes

**Race Eve Checklist - COMPLETE TODAY**

- [ ] 10 energy gels counted and ready
- [ ] Hydration system prepared
- [ ] Anti-chafe products ready
- [ ] Race bib and safety pins

- [ ] Alarm set (multiple!)
- [ ] Weather forecast checked - final clothing decision

- [ ] Post-race clothing bag packed

**Evening Routine**
- Light dinner by 6:00 PM
- Avoid alcohol completely
- Avoid new/unusual foods
- Stay off feet as much as possible
- Avoid excessive standing/walking
- Relax and watch a movie or read
- No intense activity

**Sleep Priority**
- **BED BY 8:30-9:00 PM**
- Sleep target: 9-10 hours
- Don't worry if you sleep poorly - race day adrenaline will carry you
- The sleep 2 nights before is more important
- Dark room, cool temperature
- No screen time after 8:00 PM
- Gentle breathing exercises if anxious

- "I trust my training and my body"
- "I will run my race, my pace"
- "I am strong, ready, and confident"
- "Tomorrow I become a marathoner"

- Visualize strong start, controlled pace

- Imagine pushing through tough moments

**Race Day Strategy Review:**
- Start conservatively - first 5km easy
- Build to marathon pace kilometers 6-10
- Hold steady marathon pace (5:38-5:42/km) through 35km
- Final 7km - dig deep and finish strong
- Gel every 20 minutes starting at 40 minutes
- Hydrate at every aid station
- Stay mentally present and positive

**What NOT to Do Tonight:**

- No new foods
- No intense stretching or massage
- No excessive carb loading (already done)
- No overthinking or anxiety spiral
- No staying up late
- No excessive hydration (bathroom trips)

## Day 6 - Saturday (Week 12, Day 6)

# 🏁 RACE DAY - MARATHON 🏁

### WAKE UP: 3.5 hours before race start

**Immediate Morning Routine (First 15 minutes)**
- Bathroom routine
- Drink 250ml water with electrolyte tab
- Light stretching in bed (5 minutes, very gentle)
- Check weather final time
- Get dressed in prepared race clothes

**Race Morning Breakfast (Tested and proven!)**
- White toast (FDC: 172687) - 3 slices (90g)
- Peanut butter, smooth (FDC: 172470) - 25g
- Banana, raw (FDC: 173944) - 120g
- Honey (FDC: 169640) - 20g
- Coffee (if part of routine) - 300ml
- Water - 500ml
- **Calories**: 565 kcal
- **Timing**: Finish 2.5 hours before race start

**Additional Pre-Race**
- 90 minutes before: 250ml water
- 60 minutes before: Banana (if hungry)
- 30 minutes before: 150ml water (final hydration)
- 15 minutes before: STOP drinking

### Race Day Timeline Example (8:00 AM Start)

**4:30 AM** - Wake up, bathroom, water
**4:45 AM** - Breakfast
**5:45 AM** - Get dressed, apply anti-chafe
**6:00 AM** - Leave for race
**6:45 AM** - Arrive at race, bag check
**7:00 AM** - Final bathroom stop
**7:15 AM** - Light dynamic warm-up (10 min easy jogging, 5 min stretching)
**7:30 AM** - Get in starting corral
**7:40 AM** - Final mental prep, positive affirmations
**7:50 AM** - Final gel check, watch ready
**8:00 AM** - RACE START! 🎉

### MARATHON RACE STRATEGY

**THE PLAN: Sub-4 Hour Marathon (Target: 3:55-3:58)**

**Kilometers 1-5: CONSERVATIVE START**
- **Pace**: 5:48-5:52/km (10-15 seconds SLOWER than goal)
- **Purpose**: Settle in, stay calm, don't get swept up in start excitement
- **Mental**: "Easy does it, long way to go"
- **Actions**:
  - Don't chase people passing you
  - Find your rhythm and breathing
  - Check form, stay relaxed
  - First gel at 20 minutes (around km 4)

**Kilometers 6-10: BUILD TO PACE**
- **Pace**: 5:42-5:45/km
- **Purpose**: Gradually increase to marathon goal pace
- **Mental**: "Getting comfortable, feeling good"
- **Actions**:
  - Progressive pace increase
  - Second gel at 40 minutes (km 7-8)
  - Check hydration at aid stations
  - Maintain relaxed form

**Kilometers 11-35: MARATHON PACE - THE CORE**
- **Pace**: 5:38-5:42/km (GOAL PACE)
- **Purpose**: Execute the race plan
- **This is what you trained for!**
- **GEL SCHEDULE (Critical!):**
  - Gel 3: 60 minutes (km 11)
  - Gel 4: 80 minutes (km 15)
  - Gel 5: 100 minutes (km 19)
  - Gel 6: 120 minutes (km 23)
  - Gel 7: 140 minutes (km 27)
  - Gel 8: 160 minutes (km 31)
  - Gel 9: 180 minutes (km 35)
- **HYDRATION**: Every aid station (every 2-3km typically)
  - Grab water/sports drink
  - Drink while running or quick walk
  - Don't skip aid stations
- **MENTAL MANTRAS**:
  - "This is my pace"
  - "Strong and steady"
  - "I trained for this"
  - "One kilometer at a time"
- **FORM CHECKS (Every 5km)**:
  - Shoulders relaxed
  - Arms at 90 degrees
  - Core engaged
  - Stride smooth and efficient
  - Breathing controlled

**Kilometers 35-40: DIG DEEP**
- **Pace**: 5:35-5:42/km (maintain or slightly faster if feeling good)
- **Purpose**: This is where the marathon begins
- **Mental**: "I am strong, I can do this"
- **Actions**:
  - Final gel at 35km if needed
  - Focus on form maintenance
  - Break it into 1km segments
  - Use crowd energy
  - Remember all your training
  - Think of everyone supporting you

**Kilometers 40-42.2: FINISH STRONG**
- **Pace**: 5:30-5:40/km (GIVE IT EVERYTHING!)
- **Purpose**: EMPTY THE TANK
- **Mental**: "I AM A MARATHONER!"
- **Actions**:
  - Increase pace if possible
  - Use spectator energy
  - Final push to finish
  - Stay strong through the line
  - CELEBRATE! 🎉

### During Race - Key Reminders

**Nutrition Execution:**
- Gel every 20 minutes starting at 40 minutes
- ALWAYS chase gel with water
- Don't skip any gels even if not hungry
- If stomach upset, take with more water

**Hydration Execution:**
- Hit every aid station (walk if needed)
- Alternate water and sports drink
- Pour some on head/body if hot
- Never skip hydration

**Pacing Discipline:**
- Trust your watch and pace
- Don't race people around you
- Ignore people passing early (they often fade)
- Run YOUR race, YOUR pace
- Stay controlled through halfway

**Mental Strategies:**
- Break race into segments (5km blocks)
- Count down kilometers after 30km
- Use positive self-talk constantly
- Think of training achievements
- Remember your "why"
- Visualize finish line

**Warning Signs (Adjust if needed):**
- Sharp pain → slow down immediately
- Severe stomach distress → walk aid station
- Dizziness → hydrate and slow pace
- Unusual heart rate → ease pace
- *But normal discomfort/fatigue is expected - push through!*

### Race Day Nutrition Plan

**Total Race Day Intake:**

**Pre-Race**: 565 kcal (breakfast)

**During Race** (~3:55 finish):
- Energy gels: 9-10 gels = 900-1000 kcal
- Sports drink: ~1.5-2 liters = 300-400 kcal
- Water: As needed
- **Total during race**: ~1,200-1,400 kcal

**Post-Race Immediate** (within 15 min):
- Finish line nutrition provided
- Chocolate milk or sports drink - 500ml
- Banana - 2 bananas
- Water - 500ml
- **Calories**: 450 kcal

**Recovery Meal #1** (60-90 min post-race):
- Pizza, pasta, or carb-heavy meal (race provides often)
- Protein source
- Fruit
- Continued hydration
- **Estimate**: 800-1000 kcal

**Rest of Day** (Evening):
- Continue eating every 2-3 hours
- High carbs, moderate protein
- Plenty of fluids
- **Total day estimate**: 4,500+ kcal

### Post-Race Recovery Protocol

**Immediate Post-Finish (First 30 minutes):**
- Keep walking - DO NOT sit or stop moving
- Collect finisher medal and gear
- Drink and eat immediately
- Find family/friends
- Put on warm, dry clothes
- Continue walking/moving

**First 2 Hours:**
- Eat recovery meal
- Hydrate aggressively (2+ liters)
- Keep moving periodically (short walks)
- Ice bath or cool shower if available
- Get compression socks on
- Elevate legs when sitting

**Rest of Race Day:**
- Continue eating every 2-3 hours
- Hydration priority (4+ liters total)
- Gentle walking only
- Foam rolling VERY gentle if at all
- Light stretching (very gentle)
- Compression garments
- Leg elevation
- Celebrate your achievement! 🎉

**Evening:**
- Easy, comfortable dinner
- Continued hydration
- Gentle stretching
- Epsom salt bath optional
- Early to bed
- **Sleep target: 9-10 hours**
- Extra pillows under feet

### RACE DAY MANTRAS

**When it gets tough (and it will), remember:**

1. "I am fully trained for this moment"
2. "I've run 32km with 20km at this pace - I can do this"
3. "One kilometer at a time"
4. "This is temporary discomfort for permanent pride"
5. "I am stronger than I think"
6. "Every step forward is a step closer"
7. "I've got this - trust the training"
8. "I didn't come this far to only come this far"
9. "Pain is temporary, finish is forever"
10. "I AM A MARATHONER!"

### FINAL THOUGHTS

**You've trained for 12 weeks for this day:**
- ✓ You've logged the miles
- ✓ You've done the long runs
- ✓ You've hit the paces
- ✓ You've tested the nutrition
- ✓ You've overcome challenges
- ✓ You're READY

**Your goal is achievable:**
- Half marathon time shows you can do this
- Training paces confirm you're ready
- You've run marathon pace in training successfully
- Your body is trained and tapered
- Your mind is prepared

**Remember:**
- Start conservatively
- Execute the plan
- Stay positive
- Trust your training
- Finish strong
- ENJOY THE EXPERIENCE!

### WEATHER CONTINGENCIES

**If Hot (>20°C):**
- Adjust goal slightly (add 30-60 seconds per km)
- Extra hydration at every aid station
- Pour water on head/body
- Respect the conditions

**If Rainy:**
- Adjust for wet footing
- Extra caution on turns
- Stay warm at start
- Embrace it - makes you tougher!

**If Windy:**
- Adjust effort (not pace) into headwind
- Use other runners as windbreak
- Take advantage of tailwind sections

### Looking Forward

**Immediate Future (Next 2 Weeks):**
- Complete rest from running (7-10 days minimum)
- Easy walking only
- Focus on recovery nutrition
- Gentle stretching and mobility
- Celebrate your achievement!

**When to Return to Running:**
- Listen to your body
- No running for at least 7-10 days
- Start with easy 20-30 minute runs
- Build back gradually
- No racing for 4-6 weeks
- Let body fully recover

**Future Goals to Consider:**
- Run another marathon (improve time?)
- Try different distance (half marathon PR?)
- Trail running adventure?
- Help train someone else?
- Join a running club?
- New challenges?
